The API returns 404 "Media not found" because the item isn't in the staging DB or is marked deleted; this happens during TalkChat preview fetch. The 404 is coming from the API itself, not from routing. Hitting that URL returns JSON `{"error":"Media not found"}`, which means that `mediaId` doesn’t exist in the **staging** DB (or it’s marked `isDeleted`). So the request is valid, but the data isn’t there.

To keep the UI from repeatedly retrying missing previews (and spamming 404s), I added a small negative-cache in `TalkChat` so any `404` preview is skipped on subsequent renders:  
```91:104:src/components/TalkChat.tsx
const [mediaPreviews, setMediaPreviews] = useState<Record<string, MediaPreview>>({})
const [missingPreviewIds, setMissingPreviewIds] = useState<Record<string, true>>({})
```
```1420:1464:src/components/TalkChat.tsx
const missing = Array.from(ids).filter((id) => !mediaPreviews[id] && !missingPreviewIds[id])
// ...
if (!res.ok) {
  if (res.status === 404) {
    setMissingPreviewIds((prev) => ({ ...prev, [id]: true }))
  }
  return null
}
```

If you want the preview to show in staging, you’ll need that `mediaId` to exist in the staging DB (or upload/seed it there).

It means the API is looking up that exact `mediaId` in the **staging database**. The staging DB doesn’t have a row for `5a167e52-6b9f-4a2c-9ac3-a76ddd53a4a7` (or it’s marked deleted), so the API returns 404.

To make the preview show up on staging, one of these must be true in the **staging** DB:

- A `media` record exists with `id = 5a167e52-6b9f-4a2c-9ac3-a76ddd53a4a7`
- `isDeleted` is false for that record
- `url` / `thumbnailUrl` are populated (so it has something to preview)

In practice that means: upload that media on staging, or seed the staging DB with that record.

+require('dotenv').config()
+const { PrismaClient } = require('@prisma/client')
+const bcrypt = require('bcryptjs')
+
+const prisma = new PrismaClient()
+
+const isSafeDatabaseUrl = (url) => {
+  const lower = url.toLowerCase()
+  return (
+    lower.includes('staging') ||
+    lower.includes('stg') ||
+    lower.includes('localhost') ||
+    lower.includes('127.0.0.1')
+  )
+}
+
+const ensureUser = async (user) => {
+  const normalizedEmail = user.email.toLowerCase()
+  const existing = await prisma.user.findUnique({ where: { email: normalizedEmail } })
+
+  if (existing) {
+    const updateData = {
+      name: user.name ?? existing.name,
+      role: user.role ?? existing.role,
+      membershipType: user.membershipType ?? existing.membershipType,
+      emailVerified: true,
+      policyAgreedAt: user.policyAgreedAt ?? existing.policyAgreedAt ?? new Date(),
+    }
+
+    if (user.avatar) updateData.avatar = user.avatar
+    if (!existing.password && user.password) updateData.password = user.password
+
+    return prisma.user.update({ where: { id: existing.id }, data: updateData })
+  }
+
+  return prisma.user.create({
+    data: {
+      email: normalizedEmail,
+      username: user.username,
+      password: user.password || '',
+      name: user.name || null,
+      avatar: user.avatar || null,
+      bio: user.bio || null,
+      role: user.role || 'VIEWER',
+      membershipType: user.membershipType || 'VIEWER',
+      emailVerified: true,
+      policyAgreedAt: user.policyAgreedAt || new Date(),
+    },
+  })
+}
+
+const ensureMedia = async (media) => {
+  const existing = await prisma.media.findFirst({
+    where: { title: media.title, userId: media.userId },
+  })
+  if (existing) return existing
+
+  return prisma.media.create({ data: media })
+}
+
+const ensureMessage = async (message) => {
+  const existing = await prisma.message.findFirst({
+    where: {
+      senderId: message.senderId,
+      receiverId: message.receiverId,
+      content: message.content,
+    },
+  })
+  if (existing) return existing
+
+  return prisma.message.create({ data: message })
+}
+
+const ensureChatMessage = async (chatMessage) => {
+  const existing = await prisma.chatMessage.findFirst({
+    where: {
+      userId: chatMessage.userId,
+      conversationId: chatMessage.conversationId,
+      content: chatMessage.content,
+    },
+  })
+  if (existing) return existing
+
+  return prisma.chatMessage.create({ data: chatMessage })
+}
+
+const ensureConversation = async (name, isGroup = false) => {
+  const existing = await prisma.conversation.findFirst({ where: { name } })
+  if (existing) return existing
+
+  return prisma.conversation.create({ data: { name, isGroup } })
+}
+
+const ensureConversationMember = async (conversationId, userId, priority = false) =>
+  prisma.conversationMember.upsert({
+    where: { conversationId_userId: { conversationId, userId } },
+    create: { conversationId, userId, priority },
+    update: { priority },
+  })
+
+const ensureMembershipPlan = async (plan) =>
+  prisma.membershipPlan.upsert({
+    where: { planId: plan.planId },
+    create: plan,
+    update: {
+      name: plan.name,
+      monthlyPrice: plan.monthlyPrice,
+      yearlyPrice: plan.yearlyPrice,
+      freeUploads: plan.freeUploads,
+      pricePerUpload: plan.pricePerUpload,
+      viewContents: plan.viewContents,
+      buyContents: plan.buyContents,
+      sellContents: plan.sellContents,
+      isActive: plan.isActive,
+      sortOrder: plan.sortOrder,
+    },
+  })
+
+const ensureGameSetting = async (setting) =>
+  prisma.gameSetting.upsert({
+    where: { gameId: setting.gameId },
+    create: setting,
+    update: { isEnabled: setting.isEnabled, sortOrder: setting.sortOrder, name: setting.name },
+  })
+
+const ensureNavbarItem = async (item) =>
+  prisma.navbarMenuSetting.upsert({
+    where: { itemKey: item.itemKey },
+    create: item,
+    update: { label: item.label, isEnabled: item.isEnabled, sortOrder: item.sortOrder },
+  })
+
+const ensureBadgeSetting = async (item) =>
+  prisma.mediaBadgeSetting.upsert({
+    where: { itemKey: item.itemKey },
+    create: item,
+    update: { label: item.label, isEnabled: item.isEnabled, sortOrder: item.sortOrder },
+  })
+

[Thinking] **Diagnosing API 500 Errors**

The 500 errors with empty responses likely indicate failures connecting to the database, possibly from missing or incorrect environment variables like DATABASE_URL or firewall changes blocking access. I recommend checking the app service environment variables, verifying database firewall rules, ensuring the connection string and SSL settings are correct, reviewing max connection limits, and testing DNS resolution. Testing the API with a curl request to /api/media can confirm if the app crashes early. Accessing Azure log streams or application logs is crucial to pinpoint the issue.
Here’s the most likely cause of today’s 500s:

**All failing endpoints (`/api/auth/session`, `/api/media`, `/api/promotions`, `/api/ui/navbar`, `/api/chat`) rely on Prisma → DB.**  
When the DB can’t be reached, those routes throw and return 500 with empty body. That matches what we’re seeing.

Why it can suddenly happen:
- **Azure Postgres firewall changed** (client IP or “Allow Azure services” toggled off).
- **DB server restart / transient outage** (common with maintenance windows).
- **App Service lost/changed env vars** (e.g., `DATABASE_URL` removed, slot swap).
- **TLS/SSL enforcement** (if SSL mode changed).
- **Connection limit reached** (too many connections from the app).

What to check right now:
1. **Azure Postgres → Connection security**  
   - Ensure “Allow Azure services” is **On** (or the App Service outbound IPs are whitelisted).
2. **App Service → Environment variables**  
   - Confirm `DATABASE_URL` exists and is correct in *staging slot*.
3. **App Service → Log stream**  
   - Look for Prisma `P1001` / connection errors or auth errors.
4. **Postgres metrics**  
   - Check if max connections or downtime.

If you want, I can walk you through the exact Azure checks or help parse the log stream output you paste here.

Your staging deploy is tied to **pushes to `develop`** (see `.github/workflows/azure-deploy-staging.yml`), so in this setup:

- **`develop`** → *triggers staging deployment*
- **`staging`** → separate branch you keep in sync, but it isn’t the one the staging workflow listens to
- **`main`** → production (manual deploy)

So yes, `develop` is different from `staging`, but your staging deployment currently uses `develop`. If you want staging to follow the `staging` branch instead, I can change the workflow to trigger on `staging` and stop using `develop`.

**Clarifying open chat records disappearance**

The open chat records panel vanished because the chat records fetching now relies solely on `/api/chat/conversations`, which returns an empty list for open chats; the UI treats this as success and doesn't fall back to the legacy `/api/chat/records` endpoint where public history resides. This causes an empty records list despite existing messages. Including the current fallback logic snippet highlights this behavior and suggests restoring fallback would fix the issue.
The open chat records disappeared because the UI switched to the new conversations endpoint and treated an empty response as “success,” so it never fell back to the legacy open‑chat records source. In practice, `/api/chat/conversations` returns an empty list for public/open chat, so the records list was populated with nothing and the open‑chat history panel went blank even though open messages exist. The current logic fixes this by only short‑circuiting when conversations are actually returned and otherwise falling back to `/api/chat/records`.  

```871:904:src/components/TalkChat.tsx
// Fetch chat records (previous private chat conversations)
const fetchChatRecords = useCallback(async () => {
  if (!session?.user?.id) return
  setLoadingChatRecords(true)
  try {
    // Try to fetch conversations first (new API)
    const convRes = await fetch('/api/chat/conversations')
    if (convRes.ok) {
      const convData = await convRes.json()
      const conversations = Array.isArray(convData.conversations) ? convData.conversations : []
      if (conversations.length > 0) {
        // Transform conversations to chat records format
        const records = conversations.map((conv: any) => ({
          conversationId: conv.id,
          // ...
        }))
        setChatRecords(records)
        return
      }
    }

    // Fallback to old API
    const res = await fetch('/api/chat/records')
    if (res.ok) {
      const data = await res.json()
      setChatRecords(data.records || [])
    }
  } catch (error) {
    console.error('Error fetching chat records:', error)
  } finally {
    setLoadingChatRecords(false)
  }
}, [session?.user?.id])
```

If you want, I can also pull the exact diff around the change that introduced the empty‑response short‑circuit.
